- `#1984 engine: a reader cannot tell two records apart at its epoch floor` — **VALID**
- **Claim:** A resolve treats a record at exactly the sequence floor as `Current` by sequence alone. A sibling device's record with different bytes at that sequence is not seen as a conflict. The issue asks for a stored hash of the floor record and a new outcome that every consumer handles.
- **Evidence:**
  - `crates/engine/src/net/resolve.rs:364` maps `SequenceNotNewer { floor, sequence } if sequence == floor` to `ResolveOutcome::Current` with no comparison of the record bytes.
  - `ResolveOutcome` (`resolve.rs:184`) still has only four variants: `Adopted`, `Current`, `TrustViolation` and `NoUpdate`.
  - No floor-hash store exists. A search for `floor_hash`, `record_hash` and `floor_record` finds nothing.
  - PR #1982 (`b4b8fe572`, merged 2026-09-25 11:52Z) added `fanout_get_tied`. It also added `Resolved.tied` (`resolve.rs:284`), which lists the other records at the freshest sequence. Only the writer side reads it: `sync/drain.rs:5860`, `sync/drain.rs:6865` and `net/publish.rs`. The reader consumers that the issue names (tick root leg, focus legs, rotation, liveness hold) do not read it.
  - No ADR from 0020 to 0028 and no passage in `blueprint/engine.md` changes the equal-floor rule.
- **Note:** The body is accurate. It was filed at 03:09Z on 2026-09-25, before #1982 merged. The body could now say that `Resolved.tied` exists, because a reader-side fix can build on it. The design decision is still open: a trust violation or a new outcome. The milestone is post-cutover.

---

- `#1992 engine: report a rejected root assembly on the refresh and inbox paths as a trust violation` — **VALID (open PR #1999 fixes it)**
- **Claim:** The refresh leg and the inbox walk drop a `GateError::Rejected` from `assemble_candidate` as a silent skip, with no refusal event.
- **Evidence:**
  - `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:903-905` still has `assemble_candidate(...).await.ok()?`.
  - `crates/engine/src/grants/inbox.rs:205-209` still has `let Ok(candidate) = assemble_candidate(...) else { continue };`.
  - The reference split that the body names is in `crates/engine/src/grants/link_read.rs:202-207`: `Rejected` becomes `LinkReadRefusal::Gate` and `Seam` becomes `Unavailable`.
  - Draft PR #1999 (`fix/1992-1995-engine-followups`, opened 2026-09-25 21:36Z) contains `Closes #1992.` It adds the two simulation tests that the acceptance list asks for.
- **Note:** The file paths and symbols in the body are correct.

---

- `#1995 engine: a link's contact origin is keyed by a tag that moves at every write wave` — **VALID (open PR #1999 fixes it)**
- **Claim:** The contact book keys `LinkOrigin` by the blinded link tag. A write wave rebinds that tag through `GrantSetEntry::rebound`, so after a wave three things lose the link: the per-link contact share, `contactBudgetFull`, and the `remove_grantees` fallback.
- **Evidence:**
  - `crates/engine/src/grants/contact_store.rs:353-356` has `struct LinkOrigin { link_tag, scopes }`.
  - `link_budget_full` (`contact_store.rs:387`) matches on the tag.
  - The book encodes the origin as `linkTag` (`contact_store.rs:676`, `733`).
  - `crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs:951` `rebound` replaces `tag` with the fresh tag.
  - The sharing read calls `link_budget_full(sources, &amp;link.tag)` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:11760`).
  - The revoke fallback in `crates/engine/src/grants/cut_set.rs:192` calls `book.joined_through(&amp;link.tag)`.
  - `CommittedLink::ephemeral_identity_pk` exists (`crates/engine/src/grants/invite.rs:836`), and other code already uses it as a stable key (`facade.rs:10055`, `cut_set.rs:220`).
  - Draft PR #1999 contains `Closes #1995.` It keys the origin by a new `LinkSource::Identity`, still reads `linkTag`, and adds two simulation tests.
- **Note:** The body is correct. PR #1999 lists two known windows that remain after the fix:
  - A `linkTag` entry whose link moved before the release stays unmatched.
  - A row that a wave re-mints as an owner-signed placeholder cannot be reached by `remove_grantees`.

  The second window partly misses the third acceptance item ("a link revoke with `remove_grantees` reaches a broken row after a write wave"). Before #1999 merges, check that the item still holds for a row that the wave re-mints.</result>

<result>## Issue audit: #831, #1718 and #1735

I checked all three issues against origin/main at `8baea3c45`, which includes PR #1989. PR #1989 did not change `CONTEXT.md`, `blueprint/engine.md`, `grants/create.rs` or `content/`. At origin/main, `classify_crossing` is at `facade.rs:2756` and `InteriorAdmission::Unmeasured` is at `create.rs:672,1316`. No merged PR closes any of the three issues.

### `#831 feat(engine): sealed content-block read cache` — RESCOPE

- **Claim:** The engine has no durable cache of sealed content blocks, so a re-read fetches each leaf from a gateway again. The issue lists five policy decisions that must come first.
- **Evidence:**
  - No `BlockCache` exists and no content cache exists. No ADR from 0001 to 0028 covers this cache. The link-first ADRs (0023 to 0028) do not touch it.
  - `StoragePolicy.read_cache_ceiling_bytes` exists (`crates/engine/src/storage_policy.rs:75,92-98`, PR #878), and the staging budget subtracts it. No code outside `storage_policy.rs` reads it.
  - PR #1585 added a `LocalBlocks` seam and `read_block_local_first` (`crates/engine/src/content/read.rs:366,386`). Only `StagedBlocks` (`sync/staging.rs:263`) and `GatewayOnly` implement the seam. The staging store is a write buffer, not a read cache.
  - The owner decision on 2026-09-14 says the ADR (eviction, logout survival, seam) comes before any code. That ADR does not exist.
- **Note:** These parts of the body are out of date:
  - Item 1 is partly landed: the ceiling formula is in `StoragePolicy`.
  - Item 4 is wrong: `persist()` is called (`packages/client/src/engineClient.ts:354`, `storagePersistence.ts:17`).
  - Item 5 has a third option: a `LocalBlocks` implementation.
  - "Not in scope" is wrong: `leaf_range_for_byte_range` (`read.rs:463`) is called through `read_pinned_range` (`content/mod.rs:264`, `facade.rs:12035`).
  - The line references have moved: `read_block` is now at `read.rs:306`, and the record-cache key is at `net/resolve.rs:311,318`.

### `#1718 engine: bound the resumed interior walk the way the convergence pass bounds a fresh one` — VALID

- **Claim:** A resumed grant runs no convergence pass. Its interior walk therefore moves every node that the gate authenticates, including a child that a committed writer of the scope being left adds during the stall.
- **Evidence:**
  - The gap is still in the code. `resume_grantee_scope` (`crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs:1259`) builds `InteriorBounds` with `admits: InteriorAdmission::Unmeasured` (`:1316`). The doc on the enum still says "the bound a resume owes is not landed" (`:668-672`). The membership check `InteriorNotConverged` (`:1517-1522`) runs only for `Measured`.
  - PR #1745 fixed the epoch half of the problem: the `InteriorEpochRegressed` check (`:1530-1534`) is release-active.
  - The design still applies. ADR 0026 D7 keeps "the subtree is not converged (a fresh mint only)" and "the resume is not this grant", so the fresh mint and the resume both remain. An append (D1) re-seals nothing, so this gap does not affect it.
- **Note:**
  - PR #1835 replaced the `measured: Option&lt;..&gt;` field with the `InteriorAdmission` enum. The "mode flag" sentence must name `InteriorAdmission::Unmeasured`.
  - The reach is now wider: the link-first mint also calls the resume (`crates/engine/src/grants/invite_mint.rs:172`).
  - The line references from 2026-09-05 (`:944`, `:1252-1259`, `:3007`) have moved. The sibling test is now at `create.rs:3447`.
  - The comment of 2026-09-05 still holds: option 1 needs a pass that covers two scopes, and option 2 needs a wire-format decision.

### `#1735 engine: a scope boundary the session has not proved reads as no boundary, and the overlay reaches it` — RESCOPE

- **Claim:** At journal time, `classify_crossing` reads a boundary that the session has not proved as no boundary. The issue asks for a rule, a test, a doc fix and a `CONTEXT.md` entry.
- **Evidence:**
  - The decision of 2026-09-18 is final: the journaled crossing is a plan, and the drain decides the real crossing.
  - The doc on `classify_crossing` states the rule (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:2745`, fn at `:2756`).
  - The test exists: `a_move_journaled_before_any_walk_verdict_re_seals_and_cuts_at_the_drain` (`crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs:2031`).
  - The blueprint states the rule (`blueprint/engine.md:735-738`).
  - `CONTEXT.md` does not state it. "Cross-scope move" (`:12`) and "Op queue" (`:83`) do not say that the journaled crossing is a plan and the drain is the authority.
  - The separate residual from #1802 is tracked in #1898, which is CLOSED.
- **Note:**
  - Only the `CONTEXT.md` line remains. The body must drop "What to decide" and reduce "Acceptance" to that one line.
  - The "Body check" section cites `facade.rs` line numbers from before PR #1989. The rule is now at `:2745`.
  - The comment of 2026-09-18 says that a `CONTEXT.md` change goes through the decision corpus. The decision is already recorded, so a small docs PR can close this issue.</result>

**#1984 engine: a reader cannot tell two records apart at its epoch floor — VALID**
- **Claim:** A resolve that reads a record at exactly the sequence floor returns `Current` on the sequence alone. The resolve does not compare the bytes, so a sibling device's different record at the same sequence does not show as a conflict.
- **Evidence:**
  - At `8baea3c45`, `crates/engine/src/net/resolve.rs:364` maps `SequenceNotNewer { floor, sequence } if sequence == floor` to `ResolveOutcome::Current`. It does not compare the bytes.
  - `ResolveOutcome` (`resolve.rs:184`) has only `Adopted`, `Current`, `TrustViolation` and `NoUpdate`. No floor-record hash is stored.
  - #1982 (`b4b8fe572`) added `Resolved.tied` (`resolve.rs:284`), but only the writer reads it (`sync/drain.rs:5860`, `sync/drain.rs:6865`, `net/publish.rs`). The reader paths the issue names do not read it.
  - #1989 changed nothing in this area. No ADR from 0020 to 0028 changes the equal-floor rule.
- **Note:** The body is accurate. It can add that `Resolved.tied` now exists as a base for a reader-side fix. The decision the issue asks for, a trust violation or a new outcome, is still open.

**#1992 engine: report a rejected root assembly on the refresh and inbox paths as a trust violation — VALID (open PR #1999 fixes it)**

**#1995 engine: a link's contact origin is keyed by a tag that moves at every write wave — VALID (open PR #1999 fixes it)**
- **Claim:** The contact book records the link that admitted each contact by the link's blinded tag. A write wave moves that tag. After a wave, three things lose the link: the per-link contact share, `contactBudgetFull`, and the `remove_grantees` fallback.
- **Evidence:**

- **Note:** The body is correct. <result>## Audit: #1825, #1864, #1923, #1924

The working tree and `origin/main` are both at `8baea3c45` (#1989), so every reading below includes that PR. It is the HEAD, not 5492e9880. No merged PR closes any of the four issues. All four are open, in the `post-cutover` milestone, with the labels `v2-build` and `comp:engine`.

### `#1825 engine: classify a child-leg seal failure on the seed own epoch stamp` — RESCOPE (the gap remains)

- **Claim:** `ChildAdopter::unseal` classifies a seal failure against the scope's read-epoch floor, not against the epoch stamp of the seed. A seed stamped below the floor therefore gives a trust verdict to an honest device.
- **Evidence:**
  - `crates/engine/src/net/child.rs:154-166` still branches on `epoch_floor`. `ChildAdopter::new` (`child.rs:74`) still takes a bare seed.
  - There are 8 call sites: `facade.rs:12181`, `net/focus.rs:260`, `net/rotation.rs:3890`, and `sync/drain.rs:2360`, `5881`, `6039`, `6338`.
  - The stamp exists as `CachedSeed.floor` (`facade.rs:4830-4837`).
- **Note:** The body does not match the code today in three places:
  1. `floor::check` (`gate/floor.rs:293`) sends each record below the floor to `EpochBelowFloor`. After c53b326d0 (#1921, ADR 0021) that record goes to `read_lagging` (`child.rs:448-522`), not to `unseal`. So `unseal` now sees only records at or above the floor. In the band, only a record at exactly the floor gets the wrong verdict there.
  2. In `read_lagging`, the ratchet starts from `adopter.scope_read_seed` at `anchor.epoch` (`child.rs:505`). A seed older than the anchor derives the wrong key. `open_interior_under` then returns a trust verdict (`child.rs:272-273`). The defect is on this path too, so the fix must cover both paths.
  3. The facade path evicts each seed stamped below the floor before it serves it (`refresh_seed_floor`, `facade.rs:4867-4883`, called from `scope_read_seed`, `facade.rs:10451`). The drain paths get seeds from `walked_boundary_material`, which is "not eviction-tracked" (`facade.rs:4946`). The body must name the drain and focus sites as the paths that can still reach the band.

### `#1864 engine: sweep the three by-value check surfaces the sweep measure did not reach` — RESCOPE (the gap remains, and there is a fourth surface)

- **Claim:** `NameError`, `DefaultsReason` and `MalformedDeviceField` publish check names that have no `CHECKS` const, no exhaustive `class()` and no order test in `kat_checks.rs`.
- **Evidence:**
  - All three are unchanged: `name.rs:61`, `record_plane.rs:92` and `devices.rs:60`. `git grep CHECKS` finds nothing for them. The only mention in `kat_checks.rs` is an import of `DefaultsReason` for the `PlacementRefusal` test.
  - #1865 closed #876, not this issue. It covered ten other types.
- **Note:** `ConversionRefusal::check(self)` (`grants/conversion.rs:86`, added by 5492e9880 #1988 and 8baea3c45 #1989) is a fourth by-value surface with no pin. It is more important than the other three, because `from_check` reads these names back from the durable conversion record. Add it to the scope. Also, the collision table now covers twenty surfaces, not "ten".

### `#1923 engine: an interrupted name wave leaves an old name alive with no bound` — RESCOPE (the gap remains, and many references are stale)

- **Claim:** The write-plane name wave runs inline in the owner command. It retries at most 3 times (`MAX_ROTATION_ATTEMPTS`, `rotation/retry.rs:22`) and records no durable debt. A partial or interrupted wave therefore leaves old names open with no bound.
- **Evidence:**
  - Nothing durable records an owed wave. The only owed-debt event is still `ScopeExitCutOwed` (`facade.rs:1744`).
  - The wave loop still exits at the first error (`rotation/rotate_write.rs:701-712`).
  - ADR 0025 E6 confirms that a write revoke, a downgrade and `remove_grantees` on a write grantee each run a name wave. The design still applies.
  - #1989 (8baea3c45) states that a link revoke runs no wave. The expired-link sweep refuses to move the write plane (`facade/link_sweep.rs:15-17`). So neither of them re-drives a stalled wave.
- **Note:** Update these items in the body:
  - `rotate_on_cut` has one call site, `facade/claim_conversion.rs:392`, inside `rotate_cut`. Three callers reach it: `drive_cut` (`facade.rs:8987`), the claim conversion (`claim_conversion.rs:830`) and the link sweep (`link_sweep.rs:261`).
  - `record_cut_epoch_floor` now runs at `claim_conversion.rs:395`.
  - `downgrade_grant`/`cut_recipient` no longer exist. The path is now `change_permission` (`facade.rs:9654`) → `cut_at`, with `CutKind::Downgrade` at `facade.rs:8959`.
  - The test is now at `owner_actions.rs:7879`.
  - The body says a grant revoke has no UI. That is wrong: `ShareDialog.tsx:144` now calls `actions.revoke`.
  - All `facade.rs:8xxx` line references are stale.

### `#1924 engine: measure the name wave and the sweep, and set sweep_cadence from the measurement` — VALID

- **Claim:** The engine does not measure how long the name wave or the sweep takes to converge. `sweep_cadence` is a placeholder that no measurement has replaced.
- **Evidence:**
  - `crates/engine/Cargo.toml` has no `tracing`, `log` or metrics dependency.
  - No `Event` variant reports wave or sweep progress (`facade.rs:1668-1780`).
  - `sweep_task_factory` still discards the outcome with `let _ = run.await;` (`facade.rs:3072`).
  - `sweep_cadence` is still 900 s and is still called a placeholder (`profile.rs:92-107`).
  - `tools/perf/RESULTS.md:198-205` still says "Not recorded yet".
  - The `name_wave` load scenario still drives only register and retire (`crates/load/src/scenarios.rs:109-131`).
- **Note:**
  - The discard moved from `facade.rs:2798-2806` to `facade.rs:3072`.
  - The body says `Event` "carries failure variants only". That is wrong: it has `SnapshotUpdated`, `GranteeJoined` and `OpProgress`, but none for rotation.
  - The idle sweep keeps a converged epoch per scope, in memory only (`facade.rs:6863-6870`).
  - A `cross-client-latency` perf job exists now (`.github/workflows/perf-bench.yml:159`, #1861), but no row has landed from it.
  - The new `link_sweep_cadence` (600 s, `profile.rs:85,112`) is not called a placeholder. It stays out of scope unless the owner wants it measured too.</result>

<result>None of the three issues should be closed as a duplicate. #1880 is done. #1920 and #1956 are still valid. I re-checked each verdict against origin/main at `8baea3c45` (#1989). I made no git writes and posted nothing to GitHub.

**#1880 does not subsume #1956.** #1880 is the grantee's missing write capability, and that capability has landed. #1956 is an owner-side refresh that did not settle after a grantee write. It is a different defect, and it appeared after #1880 was fixed.

---

- `#1880 fix(engine): a write grantee cannot build inside the granted folder` — **DONE**
- Claim: The facade refuses a write grantee's write inside a granted folder (`ScopeExitRefused`), because the write plane never authors inside a grafted scope. The issue is done when the last slice is done and the staging writable-share profile runs green.
- Evidence:
  - All slices are closed as completed: #1883, #1884 (PR #1893), #1885 (PR #1900), #1888 (PR #1901), #1887 (PR #1904) and #1886 (PR #1910). #1882 is closed as not planned, as the decomposition says. The host-side issue #1868 is closed by PR #1878.
  - Follow-ups PR #1926 and PR #1930 edited `tests/web-e2e/staging/writable-share.spec.ts`. The profile is no longer held out.
  - Tag-staging run 35947080124 (2026-09-24, `48bd33e63`, v2.6.0) passed all 19 staging profiles. One of them is `writable-share.spec.ts:15 › a write grant lets a second identity build inside the folder (3.8m)`.
  - `8baea3c45` does not touch this write path.
- Note: The body still says "The profile stays held" and still cites line numbers at `5b7c0b951f`. Both are out of date. Close the issue with a comment that names run 35947080124.

---

- `#1920 fix(engine): a failed vault-pointer vouch after a root read cut still locks out a one-device owner` — **VALID**
- Claim: After a read cut of the vault root, the vault-pointer vouch can fail or the root publish can report "unconfirmed" when it did land. In both cases the durable floor rises above the vouched `minReadEpoch`. `repoint_regression` then refuses the next cold start of a device that is the owner's only device. The full fix needs an ADR.
- Evidence:
  - `crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs:494` `repoint_regression` has not changed: the read-epoch stage still compares `min_read_epoch &lt; floor` at the session root. No commit after PR #1919 changes this check.
  - The only catch-up is `catch_up_vault_pointer` (`facade.rs:6037`). It runs only at cold start (`facade.rs:5945`), and the same start that needs the vouch is the one the floor refuses. No tick-side catch-up exists.
  - No ADR in cipher-box-next `decisions/` (0001 to 0028) covers this. ADR 0022 covers cold start with a failed routing endpoint, which is a different topic.
  - The severity statement is still true: `rotateNow` is called only from `packages/client/src/facade.ts:474`, and no code in `apps/web` or `apps/desktop` calls it.
  - The new expired-link sweep in `8baea3c45` (`facade/link_sweep.rs`) walks from the vault root. But a mint on the vault root is refused (`facade.rs:3943` `grant-target-is-the-vault-root`, `facade.rs:3949` `invite-target-is-the-vault-root`), so the sweep cannot cut the vault root.
- Note: The body is accurate. It can add that `catch_up_vault_pointer` exists only at cold start.

---

- `#1956 engine: the owner manual refresh stays reconciling after a write grantee builds inside the folder` — **VALID** (one occurrence, cause not found)
- Claim: Staging E2E run 35938670706 (v2.5.0) failed on writable-share. After the grantee's write, the owner's status indicator stayed `reconciling` for 300 s. The issue asks for three things: find the cause, report a failed refresh after a bounded time, and add a gate for a refresh that stays reconciling.
- Evidence:
  - Between the failing build `ee147eac9` and the passing build `48bd33e63` there are only #1944 (a web invite-page change) and the v2.6.0 release commit. No engine fix landed in that window, so the pass in run 35947080124 does not prove a fix.
  - The three scope items are not done. `manual_refresh` (`facade.rs:10571`) awaits `pass.landed()` with no deadline. `sync/refresh.rs` has no time bound on the verdict. No gate asserts that a refresh leaves `reconciling` within a time limit.
  - `8baea3c45` does not change `sync/refresh.rs`. It adds the link sweep to the tick (`sync/tick.rs`, 600 s cadence in production, at most eight cuts per sweep), and the sweep runs under the `Running` lock. This is one more job in the tick. It is not a fix.
  - PR #1982 (`b4b8fe572`) adds lost-race rebasing. The owner and a write grantee share a name key, so this is relevant. It is a possible candidate for the stall, not a proven one.
- Note: The body says the click "never got an enabled button". That conclusion is not proven. `StatusIndicator.tsx` renders a `&lt;button&gt;` with no `disabled` state. Playwright was therefore waiting for visibility or stability, or the page did not respond. A label that flips between "synced" and "syncing..." changes the button width, and that change can block the stability check. The body should state that the cause of the wait is unknown. The line reference `writable-share.spec.ts:37` still matches `await ownerFiles.status.click();`.</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I wrote ADR 0030. It has 11 decisions, D1 to D11. Every Dn has at least one test, but four have a gap. The blueprint and the code do not contradict each other on any rule; I found two code asymmetries and two out-of-date glossary entries.

Output: `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0030-a-record-only-the-owner-reads-seals-in-hpke-auth-mode-to-the-owner-itself.md` (455 lines, markdownlint clean).

**Decisions:**
- **D1 (general rule):** a structure that the owner alone writes and alone reads seals in HPKE auth mode, with the owner as both sender and recipient. The owner blob and the owner write blob stay in base mode, because a rotator (who can be a write grantee) re-seals them. That split is also why #39 D2 does not conflict: #39 D2 rejects auth mode keyed to the write plane for structures with many writers, and this rule keys it to the owner's own key and uses it only where the owner is the one writer.
- **D2 to D9** cover the 12 gap rows: the settings name edge, the settings record and its auth mode, the op record, its frozen clear header, its auth mode, the content key blob and its auth mode.
- **D10** closes the #27 D6 lag (the write-plane history link). **D11** closes the ADR 0006 lag (owner-local kinds `0x04` and `0x05`).
- Consequences 4 and 5 state how ADR 0006 and #27 D6 (and #27 D3) now read.
- Decided by the owner, per the source issues: D2 and D3 (FSM1/cipher-box#825, 2026-07-28), D5 (FSM1/cipher-box#832, 2026-07-28) and D8 (FSM1/cipher-box#818, 2026-07-27). The rest are recorded as "landed with".
- I dropped a draft D2 ("auth mode does not make the recipient key private"). It was not a rule from the audit entry, and D4 already covers it.

**Test gaps (findings):**
- **D1:** nothing checks that the owner blob and the owner write blob stay in base mode, except their accept vectors.

**Blueprint against the code:** the code matches the blueprint for every rule. There are two conflicts inside the blueprint, and one wording tension:
1. **The family rule is wider than the corpus.** `core.md` "Bin index" says that a structure with one reader seals symmetrically. The code (`halt_for_bin_load` gives `Halt::Attempt`) matches both. D9 records the reading "no uncharged retry" (residual E8).

**An accepted trap:** `publish_bin_index` raises the mint counter before it draws the nonce and before the PUT. On a single-device account, a genesis bin publish that fails after the mint leaves the device with `StrandedMint`. The device does not try again, and its first soft delete dead-letters until a second device publishes the record. The blueprint states this, and residual E5 records it.

- **D1:** no test asserts that an `External` write's record head block goes to the hosted store, and no suite runs that upload against the real API.

**Disagreements between the blueprint and the code** (the ADR records the blueprint and states each one as a residual):
1. **E1 is a likely live defect.** Under `External`, the rules conflict:
   - D1 sends every record head block to the hosted upload endpoint in every mode (`crates/engine/src/net/record_publish.rs`).
   - D11 sets `byo=true` for an `External` account, in the command-time pre-flight before the drain runs.
   - The API refuses any hosted upload from a `byo=true` account with a 409 (`apps/api/src/content/content.service.ts`).

   So every record publish under `External`, the settings record publish included, gets a 409 and uses up the op's attempts.
   - The engine test fake does not model the 409 (`crates/engine/src/testkit/account.rs`).
   - The contract suite checks the 409 only for a content block (`quota_is_hosted_authoritative_and_byo_advisory`).
   - A member can reach `External` from the web settings form.
   - `blueprint/api.md` says "BYO/dual bytes bypass the API entirely", and the `CONTEXT.md` "Advisory pin row" entry says the same. Both disagree with D1.
2. **E2:** the blueprint says the external retry budget belongs to the op. The code gives each drain pass a new 3-attempt budget (`MirrorLeg::new()` in `upload_blocks`, `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs`). The hosted leg has no retry loop of its own; its retries come from the op's attempt budget (`ATTEMPT_BUDGET`). A config refusal that no retry can fix still uses up the mirror's attempts.
3. **E4:** section 6 of the FSM1/cipher-box#822 resolution said retire and prune unpin both legs. That rule is in neither the blueprint nor the code, so bytes on the member's own provider are never unpinned.
4. **E7 (minor):** the contract-suite HTTP client (`crates/contract/src/lib.rs`) follows redirects, which breaks the D15 no-downgrade rule. It is test code, not shipped code.

E3 is a gap, not a disagreement: on a fresh device where the settings record is held back, a `Dual` account can silently drop to one copy. The blueprint's "Vault settings load" section owns that case.</result>

**What the decisions cover:**
- **General rule (D1):** every attacker-sized field has a frozen bound that decode and encode enforce with the same verdict. It cites AGENTS.md rule 8 and does not restate it.
- **Count bounds (D2–D5):** grant-section collections, commitment entries, the child-scope index and the ledger rows.
- **Size bounds (D6–D9):** the write-plane history link (512 bytes), the write-body total, the grant-section total, and the envelope and `readSealed` bounds with the bytes each one counts.

- **No permanent refusal (D11):** a size that an attacker influences never causes a permanent refusal when the client produces a record.
- **Retention (D12):** a rotation keeps the newest 64 history links that still walk, and a sweep neither adds nor removes links. This closes the #27 "pruned as the sweep converges" lag. Consequence 4 records the amendment.
- **Freezing and canonical forms (D13–D15):** the KAT manifest `bounds` table, the base32 content-CID string codec, and X25519 key adoption.

The reason is that an empty link above write epoch 1 cuts the write-plane history chain. The ADR records the blueprint, as the brief requires. The owner must choose one; if the code stands, D6's last sentence reads "refuses before any seal".
- **E2:** the blueprint contradicts itself. `core.md` "History-link retention" says a node past the 64-link window "is not lost, since the sweep re-seals it forward". `engine.md` "sweep" says such a node is "readable by nobody", and the code and ADR 0021 agree with `engine.md`. The `core.md` sentence cannot be true, because a re-seal needs the old seed to open the body. The ADR records the `engine.md` text, and the `core.md` sentence needs an editorial fix.

Output: `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0037-no-browser-channel-carries-what-names-the-vault-and-every-boundary-value-is-bounded.md` (350 lines).

**The decisions:**
- **D1** Follower traffic uses a private port that the Service Worker brokers. A tab with no Service Worker fails closed.
- **D2** The `BroadcastChannel` carries only election and the port rendezvous: no plaintext, no key material, no user-supplied name.
- **D3** Nothing that names or measures vault content goes on the channel. The event stream goes over the private ports. This replaces the `#45` "thin mirrors over BroadcastChannel" sentence (consequence 4).
- **D4** The leader reclaims everything a dead follower held.
- **D5** A per-tab presence Web Lock detects follower death, at the cost of bfcache eligibility.
- **D6** A record GET is bounded by `maxBytes` and a deadline.
- **D7** Every `Http` request carries a deadline, set per request class.
- **D8** Ranged-read numbers cross as JS numbers, and the producer refuses a size past `MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`.

Every source is NONE, so each decision says "landed with FSM1/cipher-box#N; this ADR records it". The credentials scope is left out of the decisions as NOT-A-RULE (baseline `Http` row), as the audit said.

**Decisions with no test or only a partial one:**
- **D8:** the producer refusal (the `MAX_SAFE_SIZE` check in `open_content_stream`, `crates/wasm/src/host.rs`) has no test. Only the follower-side re-check in `engineClient.test.ts` and the `range.test.ts` 416 cases are tested.
- **D7:** no test asserts that every engine `HttpRequest` carries a deadline. `timeout_ms` is an `Option`, and the web seam treats `None` as unbounded. Today the rule holds only because every call site passes `Some` (E5).
- **D5:** the bfcache trade has no test.
- **D1:** the no-Service-Worker fail-closed rule is tested only at unit level. No browser-suite test covers it.
- **D4:** the browser test proves the lock is granted, not that the handles are released. Only the unit test proves the release.

**Where the blueprint and the code disagree (E2):** the blueprint says election and the port rendezvous ride the channel and "everything else takes a different wire". The code also posts `cb:sessionEnded` on the channel (added in FSM1/cipher-box#1502, posted from `packages/client/src/engineClient.ts`). The message names nothing and measures nothing, so D3 still holds, but D2's "nothing else" clause does not. The ADR records the blueprint. The owner needs to either name session-end as a third channel role or move it. The `leaderRelay.ts` header says "election and the port rendezvous only" and is wrong in the same way.

**One other finding (E4), outside this ADR's scope:** Web Lock names can be read by anything on the origin. The device-identity lock in `apps/web/src/auth/deviceIdentity.ts` is named with an unsalted SHA-256 of the sign-in subject, which for email sign-in is the email address. It identifies the member, not vault content.</result>

**Output:** `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0038-the-hosted-store-pins-content-only-under-the-address-the-client-declares.md`

**Count of Dn:** 8.
- **D1:** the client declares the address, and the API pins under exactly that address. A mismatch or a shape other than `raw`/`dag-cbor` BLAKE3-256 gets a permanent 400.
- **D2:** the declared address travels in the `X-Content-Cid` header, never in the URL.
- **D3:** one request carries one block, and every path that does not pin the block removes it.
- **D4:** the transport cap is the block ceiling, and an over-size body gets a permanent 413 with the code `UPLOAD_TOO_LARGE`. The 2 MiB value is cited as the external Bitswap and Kubo limit, not stated as a rule.
- **D5:** pins are direct, not recursive.
- **D6:** the contract suite proves the pinned address equals the declared one against a real API and Kubo.
- **D7:** the plaintext chunk is 1,048,536 bytes and seals to a 1 MiB block. Owner-decided on 2026-07-28 in FSM1/cipher-box#820 under map #813.
- **D8:** the DAG is a flat root with a format version, and its link list caps the file size. Owner-decided on 2026-07-28 in FSM1/cipher-box#820 under map #813.

D1 to D6 have no decision source and landed with FSM1/cipher-box#912. D7 and D8 landed with FSM1/cipher-box#877.

**Dn with no test:**
- **D5:** nothing fails if the pin becomes recursive. The unit-test stub in `apps/api/src/registry/pin-store.test.ts` records only the URL path, so `recursive=false` is never checked, and no live test checks the pin type.
- **Partial gaps:**
  - **D3:** only a stubbed Kubo proves that a refused block leaves the datastore. The contract suite proves only that the quota charge is refunded.
  - **D4:** the 413 integration test sets `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES=16`, so no test ties the default cap to the block ceiling.
  - **D1:** no unit test checks the `cid-codec`, `mhtype=blake3` and `mhlen=32` parameters of `block/put`. Only the contract suite's gateway round-trip covers them.

**Disagreements between the blueprint and the code:**
1. **File-size cap (E1).** `blueprint/engine.md` ("Chunking and retention") says the flat root caps a file at about 107.78 GiB, which was 110,375 links under the old 4 MiB record ceiling. FSM1/cipher-box#1310 (2026-08-19) lowered the record ceiling to the 2 MiB block ceiling. The code (`crates/engine/src/content/limits.rs`) and the KAT vectors (`crates/engine/kat/vectors/content/dag_capacity_*.json`) now enforce 55,187 links, about 53.89 GiB. The ADR records the blueprint figure as the brief requires. It also says the code value is the only one the hosted store can hold, and that correcting the number needs your decision.
2. **Bare tracker reference.** `blueprint/engine.md` cites "(#820)" without the repository. Consequence 8 replaces it with the ADR 0038 citation.

**Other honest residuals in the ADR:**
- **E2:** a re-upload of an address the account already holds returns 201 without checking the bytes. Nothing is written.
- **E3:** a mismatched upload can remove a block that a concurrent upload wrote but has not yet pinned. The effect is a retryable 503 only.
- **E4:** the mismatch 400 carries no `code`, so the engine spends its retry budget before it dead-letters the op.
- **E5:** `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` is operator configuration, so D4 holds only at the default.
- **E6:** three rules depend on Kubo's own behaviour, and only the contract suite runs a real Kubo.
- **E7:** the plaintext root reveals chunk count and plaintext size to anyone who holds a `contentCid`.</result>

**E1 defect**

The defect is real. All three claims hold at origin/main:

- **(a)** `publish_record` always uploads the head to the hosted endpoint, whatever the placement mode (`crates/engine/src/net/record_publish.rs:233-235`: `api.upload(&amp;request.head.cid, &amp;request.head.block)`, which POSTs `/content/upload`). Every record publish goes through it: the drain at `drain.rs:6082`, the settings save at `settings.rs:728`, the bin index, rotation and provisioning.
- **(b)** The command-time pre-flight sets `byo=true` for an `External` placement (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:10843-10847`: `if !self.byo_reconciled.get() &amp;&amp; quota.advisory == hosted_leg &amp;&amp; api.set_byo(!hosted_leg)`). `has_hosted_leg` is false for `External` (`settings.rs:217-218`). The pre-flight runs from `begin_write` (`facade.rs:10750`), and this is the only place the engine sets the flag.
- **(c)** The API refuses every hosted upload from a `byo=true` account (`apps/api/src/content/content.service.ts:245-246`: `if (user.byo) { throw new ConflictException('Hosted ingress is unavailable for BYO accounts'); }`). The check is reached on every upload, before the idempotent short-circuit, with no codec exemption.
- **Tests:** the engine test fake does not model the 409 (`crates/engine/src/testkit/account.rs:406-430` never reads the flag). `an_external_write_places_every_block_on_the_members_node_and_none_on_the_hosted_store` passes only because of that. The contract test proves the 409 for a content leaf only.
- **Web:** `VaultSettingsForm.tsx:29` offers `'external'`, and `SettingsPage.tsx:87` renders the form.

What a member sees, in `External` mode over Kubo (`External` over PSA or Pinata is already refused):

1. The save that selects `External` works, because `byo` is still false.
2. The first file write sets `byo=true`. The drain puts the blocks on the member's node, then gets a 409 on the file's record head. The drain counts each 409 as a failed attempt, and after 5 attempts (`ATTEMPT_BUDGET`) it dead-letters the op with `AttemptsExhausted`. The file never publishes.
3. The queue is strict FIFO, so every later op (folder create, rename, move, delete, and every file write) hits the same 409 and dead-letters in turn. Bin-index and rotation publishes fail the same way.
4. A settings save is not a drain op. It fails at once with `EngineError::Seam` "the settings record did not reach the record plane" (`facade.rs:2232`). So the claim that the settings publish exhausts the op's attempts is imprecise.
5. This includes a save that switches back to `Hosted`, so the device cannot get out of this state from the app. Only another device that still holds a `Hosted` or `Dual` placement and starts a file write would clear the flag.

No open cipher-box issue covers this; four searches, including the one you gave, found nothing matching. The one BYO-related open issue, #959, is a load-test task and is unrelated.</result>

**Needs the writer**

1. **E1 and D6: the code is right and the blueprint sentence is out of date.** core.md "Write-body" says a re-seal "drops it rather than failing". Since FSM1/cipher-box#1748, `reseal_scope_root` refuses first with `CarriedWriteHistoryLinkTooLarge` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs:821`). Dropping the link would publish an empty link above write epoch 1, which the `Genesis` arm refuses. The decoder already refuses such a link, so no gate-passed record reaches this refusal, and D11 still holds.
   - Recommendation: D6 records the refusal, and D11 bullet 3 cites E6's per-link drop instead of D6.
   - The core.md sentence needs an editorial fix.
   - The doc comment at `crates/engine/src/content/limits.rs:59-61` ("exactly as an over-long write history link already is") has been false since #1748. That is a small code defect.
2. **E2: `engine.md` is right and the `core.md` sentence is wrong.** core.md says a node past the window "is not lost, since the sweep re-seals it forward from the scope's current seed". engine.md says it is "readable by nobody". The code doc (`reseal.rs:61-65`) and the ADR 0021 table agree with engine.md. A re-seal needs the old seed, and no kept link reaches it. D12 is correct as drafted; E2 should say directly that core.md is wrong.
3. **Consequence 3 is incomplete.** `CONTEXT.md` "History link" says a new grantee "reads every earlier epoch". Under D12 that is only the epochs inside the 64-rotation window. This is the same overstatement E5 records for ADR 0026 D6, so it needs an editorial fix or a reason why it needs none.
4. **E7 is a real code defect, and worse than the draft says.** `encode_content_cid_str` (`crates/core/src/content/cid.rs:136-140`) uses a release-active `assert!`, which AGENTS.md rule 8 forbids. Attacker-authored input can reach it:
   - `Version::from_value` (`crates/core/src/seal/body.rs:212`) accepts any `contentCid` bytes.
   - `open_content_root` (`crates/engine/src/content/mod.rs:220`) encodes the adopted head version's CID with no `is_wellformed_content_cid` check. The rotation path does check (`net/rotation.rs:4440`).
   - So a committed write grantee can publish a file version with a malformed `contentCid`, and every reader who opens that file panics.
   - I found this by reading the code; no test was run.
   - E7 must say this is a panic a peer can trigger. It needs a code issue: either the encoder returns `Result`, or the `Version` decode checks the CID.

Item 5 in the review file is not a blocker. It confirms E6: `MAX_RETAINED_HISTORY_LINK_BYTES` = 256 in `limits.rs:72`, from #1594, is stated in no blueprint sentence. If item 1 moves D11 bullet 3 onto it, E6 must be updated to say a Dn now cites it.

<result>Verdict: RETURN

Needs the writer:

1. **D1 and the title state a rule for every owner-only record, and the bin index breaks it.** D1 says: "A structure the owner alone authors and alone reads seals in HPKE auth mode to the owner itself." But the `blueprint/core.md` "Bin index" section (line 536) says: "a structure whose readership is exactly one, forever, seals symmetrically under its own login-secret edge, because possession of the key is already the author proof; a structure a public half must address seals HPKE-to-self." The bin index (`0x0f`, ADR 0010) is written and read only by the owner, and it does not use auth mode. The choice between a symmetric seal and HPKE belongs to candidate 3. D1 must decide only the HPKE mode, for example: "a structure that seals HPKE to the owner's own `enc-subkey`, and that the owner alone legitimately authors, seals in auth mode". The title must change to match, and one sentence should point to the bin index as the symmetric case.
2. **D11 and consequence 4 list four owner-local kinds, but the code has seven.** The blueprint lists `0x01`, `0x02`, `0x04` and `0x05`. `crates/core/kat/manifest.json` also has `scope-exit-debt` (6, FSM1/cipher-box#1755), `pending-conversions` (7, FSM1/cipher-box#1988) and `grantee-names` (8, FSM1/cipher-box#1987). The writer must add a residual about this gap between the blueprint and the code, and must stop presenting the list as closed. The general rule in D11 already covers the three new kinds.
3. **Consequence 4 misses a sentence in ADR 0006 that this ADR reverses.** The third rejected alternative in ADR 0006 says: "they carry sender authentication and recipient binding that owner-local state has no use for". D11 gives owner-local state sender authentication, and the code has done so since FSM1/cipher-box#1215. Consequence 4 must say how that sentence reads now: the reason not to reuse those formats is their network-shaped framing and versioning, not sender authentication.

The crypto claims you asked me to check are correct:

- **D1 split and #39 D2:** #39 D2 rejects only "HPKE auth-mode keyed to the write plane". The draft keys auth mode to the owner's `enc-subkey`, so there is no conflict. The code agrees: `reseal.rs:910,934` seals the owner blob and the owner write blob in base mode, and any rotator can do it, a write grantee included.
- **E3:** `GRANT_HPKE_INFO` is `b""`, and the grant family shares it. The link stays separate through tag `0x0e` in the AAD and the auth-mode key schedule.
- **D6:** `v` is in the AAD. The version check runs before decryption, so the gap in the tests is real, but an in-crate test could cover it.
- **E4:** confirmed.

Output: `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0040-the-fuse-projection-seals-its-spill-files-and-never-logs-a-name.md`

The five rules:
- **D1:** the FUSE-T SMB mount sets `noattrcache`, and every remote change fires push invalidation.
- **D2:** a hosted-quota refusal returns `EDQUOT`. It is recorded as decided on 2026-07-28 in FSM1/cipher-box#841 under the #813 map, as the v4 ruling says. SUMMARY section 1 still asks you to confirm that ruling.
- **D3:** the spill file seals under the `crates/core` AEAD, which is the one direct use of `crates/core` in `crates/fuse`.
- **D4:** `getattr` puts the file itself in view. `MAX_FOCUS_FILES` bounds the burst per tick, and the sibling-set exposure is accepted.
- **D5:** no default `Filesystem` body in the vendored `fuser` writes a name to a log record.

**Dn with no test, or only part-tested:**
- **D1:** no CI test proves the smbfs behaviour itself. Only hardware gate 1 in `tools/hw-gates/RESULTS.md` proves it, and that is not CI.
- **D2:** no `FUSE Op Core` test sends an over-quota write through a mount operation to `EDQUOT` end to end. Only the errno table tests and the engine command-time test exist.
- **D3:** no test or lint proves that the AEAD is the only use of `crates/core`.
- **D4:** no test proves that an entry the bound evicts is queued again on the next stat. The accepted exposure is a design statement, so it has no test.
- **D5:** the macOS-only arms of `default_filesystem_ops_never_log_a_name` (`setvolname`, `exchange`) never run in CI. `cargo test -p fuser` runs only on `ubuntu-latest`, and the macOS adapter leg does not test `fuser`.

**Where the blueprint and the code disagree (the ADR records the blueprint each time):**
1. **Second trigger for the D4 burst.** Since FSM1/cipher-box#1785 and #1802, the tick also queues every size-less file child of a folder in view (`queue_unprojected_children` in `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`). A `lookup` that returns a size-less file also queues it (`crates/fuse/src/ops.rs`). A listing therefore emits the burst with no `getattr` at all, and the `desktop.md` "Freshness" bullet does not say so. This is residual E1.
2. **Other uses of `crates/core`.** The "Component map" says the AEAD is the one direct use of `crates/core`. `crates/fuse/src/adapter.rs` and `ops.rs` also import `cipherbox_core::codec::RedactedText`. It is not crypto, but the sentence is no longer accurate. This is residual E2.
3. **Spill key held by the FS layer.** The "Doctrine" section says the FS layer holds no keys, but the spill key lives in FS memory. The "write" bullet is the correct statement, and the ADR records it. This is also in E2.

**Other honest limits in the Residuals section:**
- The spill key is not locked in memory, so a swap file or hibernation image can hold it.
- A quota refusal that comes after the journal ack becomes the `Blocked` state, not `EDQUOT`.
- On the SMB backend, a missed invalidation has no backstop.
- D5 covers the vendored `fuser` only. WinFsp goes through `winfsp-rs`, which has no log assertion.
- `apps/desktop` installs no log backend today, so no log record reaches a sink yet.
- A redacted name still shows its byte length.

**Needs the writer**

1. **The ADR 0030 and ADR 0031 drafts contradict each other.**
   - 0030 D1 says: "A structure the owner alone authors and alone reads seals in HPKE auth mode to the owner itself."
   - The 0030 Context says "Five structures have one author and one reader", and that list leaves out the bin index. 0030 consequence 11 gives every new owner-only structure a base-mode forgery vector.
   - 0031 D1 says the opposite: "a structure whose readership is exactly one, for ever, seals symmetrically under its own login-secret edge".
   - The two drafts agree on which structure uses which seal. Only the general rules conflict. The source is `core.md:536`-`:540`, which is wider than the "Structure-tag registry" section of `core.md`.
   - Recommendation: 0030 D1 names the bin index as the one exception and cites ADR 0031. 0031 D1 gives only the reason for the bin: the login-secret key proves the author, and no public half addresses the record. 0031 E1 becomes a consequence that rewords `core.md` "Bin index", and the owner accepts that rewording through the ADR process.
2. **E5 is real, and the blueprint states only part of it.**
   - **Mechanism:** `bin_index.rs:322` raises the mint counter before the nonce draw (`:323`) and the PUT (`:334`). After that, `holds_a_bin_index_mark` (`:253`-`:264`) skips every later genesis attempt on that device. `unresolved_reason` (`record_plane.rs:137`) returns `StrandedMint`, and `drain.rs:210` dead-letters every soft delete on the device, not only the first one.
   - **What the blueprint says:** `engine.md:455`-`:458` accepts the outcome: "the member is told the state and may hard-delete instead".
   - **What the blueprint does not say:**
     - A failed genesis publish leads to this state.
     - The state is permanent. The FSM1/cipher-box#1742 body says the account "could never soft-delete again, and nothing said why", and that PR fixed only the second half.
     - An entropy failure after the mint also strands the device. Moving the nonce draw before the mint, or persisting the revision just before the PUT, would narrow this.
   - The decision source is NONE: FSM1/cipher-box#1675 lists options but decides none. Put this to the owner as an open acceptance, not as a settled decision.
3. **D9 overlaps candidate 15.** D9's clause "the drain charges a verdict against the attempt budget … holds the op uncharged only for availability" restates `engine.md:453`-`:457`. That text belongs to the candidate 15 row "The queue head never waits on the bin plane in silence". The two writers must agree which ADR states it, and the other ADR cites it.
4. **Minor, on the ADR 0007 extension.** Under D2, two concurrent first runs publish two different empty records at sequence 1. Consequence 6 should say in one sentence that ADR 0007 consequence 6 (semantically identical records) covers this.
5. **For the 0030 writer.** Its Gate places `crates/engine` in `Workspace tests (Linux)`, but `ci-rust.yml:63` excludes `cipherbox-engine` from that job. The engine tests run in `Engine simulation tests` (`ci-rust.yml:77`-`:106`), as the 0031 Gate says.

**Needs the writer:** none.

**E1, the file-size cap: confirmed. The blueprint sentence is wrong.**
- `engine.md` says "~107.78 GiB". The FSM1/cipher-box#820 resolution calculated that value as 110,375 links under the 4 MiB `MAX_RESOLVED_RECORD_BYTES` of that time. It was a derived value. The owner decided "flat", with the resulting maximum stated.
- FSM1/cipher-box#1310 (2026-08-19) set that ceiling to the 2 MiB block ceiling. The reason: `block/put` refuses larger blocks, so a root between 2 MiB and 4 MiB could be written but not pinned. The same PR regenerated the KAT capacity vectors from 110,375 links to 55,187.
- The code now enforces 55,187 links, 57,865,556,232 bytes, about 53.89 GiB (`limits.rs:32`, `dag_capacity_accept.json`, `dag_capacity_reject.json` at 55,188). I checked the arithmetic for both values.
- The hosted store could never reach 107.78 GiB. The blueprint value is therefore an editorial fix, not a question for the owner.

- D8 now states the code value.
- The old E1 is now consequence 9: "`engine.md` 'Chunking and retention' replaces ~107.78 GiB with ~53.89 GiB".
- The residuals are renumbered, so **the 400 residual you called E4 is now E3**.

- `content.service.ts:199-200` returns a 400 with only a message, and no `code`.
- `classify_upload` in `drain.rs` counts a 400 against the attempt budget (`Halt::UploadAttempt`). The op dead-letters only when the budget is spent. The test `a_refusal_of_these_bytes_costs_an_attempt_and_never_dead_letters_on_sight` proves this.
- This agrees with the ADR 0029 draft. It has no D rule on refusal codes, and its E1 cites the same test for the 409.

## Needs the writer

1. **D4 records a rule that the code does not implement, and no issue tracks it.** Your E1 finding is correct.
   - The blueprint says: "Device approval is requester-only through the cutover" (`desktop.md:299`).
   - In the code, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs:45-54` registers no rendezvous command, and the desktop webview holds no device identity key.
   - FSM1/cipher-box#1764 says: "The requester screen needs a rendezvous seam this host does not have."
   - The search you gave finds only FSM1/cipher-box#1514 and its umbrella FSM1/cipher-box#1262. #1514 is titled "desktop: implement the device-approval approver role" and is in the `post-cutover` milestone. Its body states the reverse of the code: "Desktop currently ships as a device-approval requester only". Only its 2026-09-05 comment mentions the requester leg.
   - The cutover was 2026-09-13, so the "through the cutover" window closed and the rule never shipped.
   - The owner must choose one of two options:
     - File a requester issue, or re-scope #1514 and correct its body, and keep D4 as written.
     - Rule that the as-built 2026-08-25 position (no rendezvous role) is the decision. Then D4, alternative (c), consequence 5 and the blueprint bullet need rewording.

2. **Privacy finding (E2, separate from the ADR text): `identity_subjects.identifier_display` is written but never read.**
   - It is written at every first mint. The only code that reads `identifierDisplay` (`auth.service.ts:204`) reads from `auth_methods`, not from this table.
   - The column keeps the full email domain plus the first two characters of the email name, or a wallet address's first 6 and last 4 characters.
   - The hash beside it is unsalted SHA-256. A database reader can recover a wallet address by hashing public on-chain addresses that match the prefix and suffix. The reader can also test email guesses within the known domain.
   - `account_devices.identity_subject_id` links each subject to a `user_id`, so the partial identifier is tied to an account.
   - `auth_methods` stores the same hash-plus-display pair, which is outside this ADR.
   - Proposal: E2 states that the column has no reader, and a v2.0.0 issue drops it or salts the hash. This is an owner call.

3. **A code comment keeps the citation that FSM1/cipher-box#2005 removed from the blueprint.** `packages/auth-ui/src/index.ts:1-2` still cites "(ADR 0008 D3)". Either add a consequence that points it at ADR 0039, or leave it to the editorial follow-up.

4. **A suggested new residual; E3 covers only the operator.**
   - The identity token is a 300-second bearer that the API accepts more than once.
   - At registration, the API does not check that the token belongs to the session's account.
   - Someone with a full session on their own account and another member's leaked identity token could claim that member's subject first. D3 would then refuse that member's own device registrations, and the member's new devices would post their approval requests to the wrong account.
   - This blocks device approval but discloses no key: a factor for another account's key cannot open the member's account.

Besides these, every test name cited in the Gate exists at `origin/main`, every "no test" claim holds, and all rule rows are covered. I also ran `git fetch` on the cipher-box checkout to read the current `origin/main`. It changed only the remote-tracking refs.</result>

That is what the code does now, since `is_uncuttable` has no "adopted" state. The reason is that a re-author cannot tell an honest newer field from marked padding. No earlier decision records this answer, so the ADR states that it is proposed for you to accept. It is not recorded as already decided. - D8 has only the unconditional-keep test. The code has no "not seen adopted" state to test.

**Blueprint vs code:** I found no conflict, but E3 records two gaps:
- The code applies the two reserved names at the top level only. Inside `epochTag` they are ordinary, cuttable and not budgeted (`a_protocol_bearing_name_inside_the_epoch_tag_is_still_cuttable`). The blueprint does not say this.
- The blueprint says the encode "refuses only what the typed fields alone overflow". The code also counts the uncuttable carried fields. The next blueprint sentence makes those fields uncuttable, so the two sentences together match the code.</result>

- **D1, the crossing is a plan and the drain decides:** decided by the owner on 2026-09-18 in FSM1/cipher-box#1735. It cites FSM1/cipher-box#1828 for the blueprint and FSM1/cipher-box#2002 for the `CONTEXT.md` line, both landed. Option 1 (durable proved scope roots per account) is recorded as rejected, with its reason.

- **D7, the conditional edit:** NONE; FSM1/cipher-box#1123.

- **D5:** unit tests cover the stamp itself. No test shows that the overlay and the drain's publish plan stamp the same nodes, and no test goes through all the op kinds, which the FSM1/cipher-box#830 resolution asked for.
- **D6:** the single-entry `move` is tested. Nothing tests the staged two-leg move (see the first disagreement below) when its second leg fails to journal.

**Where the blueprint and the code disagree.** The ADR records the blueprint in both cases and names the code path in a residual.
1. **E2, a move between two shared folders:** the blueprint says one POSIX rename is exactly one `move`, "journaled or none of it is". Since FSM1/cipher-box#1765, a move between two shared folders is journaled as two entries instead: a parking relink into the vault-root scope, then the arriving op (`relocation_legs` and `stage_legs_and_notify` in `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`).
   - Undoing the first entry when the second fails is best-effort only. If that fails, or a tick drains the parking leg first, the subtree is left parked in the vault-root scope.
   - Another device that polls between the two ticks also sees it parked.
   - No blueprint text describes this two-step plan.
2. **E3, the shared stamping function:** the blueprint says the overlay stamps "through the one function the drain's publish plan shares". In the code, only the overlay calls `Op::stamp_authored` (`crates/engine/src/sync/op.rs`). The drain reads `applied.op.authored_at` directly at each site in `drain.rs` and `author.rs`. The values match today, but nothing forces them to stay in step.
   - Also, `prune`, `restoreVersion` and `deleteVersion` each write the target's next record but get no stamp, which strains the rule "every op but a delete". Those three kinds are outside the blueprint's six-op list.

**Other residuals:**
- **E1:** a crossing journaled before the first boundary walk can name a boundary the session had not proved. Nobody can observe this. The only cost is availability: the op waits at the queue head, uncharged, until the walk proves the boundary.
- **E4:** `authored_at` is a time the client sets, so a skewed client publishes a skewed modified time.
- **E5:** the owner tag is readable in the clear, and a record with a corrupted tag is kept forever as if it were another identity's.
- **E6:** a record whose sealed body no longer opens is removed, and its staged version is lost.
- **E7:** a grant created after a move was queued can dead-letter that move.</result>

In the code, only tray "Sync Now" does. The TTL check (`ttl_check` in `crates/fuse/src/ops.rs`, via `note_focus_access`) only adds the node to the focus window for the next tick. No desktop code listens for reconnect or wake. The ADR records the blueprint.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I wrote ADR 0046, with six rules (D1 to D6), and found a likely real bug: files between about 1.6 GiB and about 53.89 GiB probably cannot publish (E1 below). I found it by reading the code and did not run it.

**Output:** `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0046-the-registry-counts-references-per-record-and-caps-every-batch-at-1000.md` (360 lines, markdownlint clean).

**The six rules:**
- **D1:** the registry keeps one reference per record that names a CID, and a retire scoped to one record drops only that record's references. This one was decided by the owner on 2026-08-31 in FSM1/cipher-box#1176.
- **D2:** retire takes `[{ipnsName?, targets[]}]`, and the batch caps its total target count at 1000.
- **D3:** both batches cap at 1000 entries, a register entry caps its CIDs at 1000, and an over-cap batch is refused whole with a 400.
- **D4:** a large version registers as several entries under one name.
- **D5:** the refusal-code rule you asked me to place here. The engine drops a queued write only when the 400 or 413 carries the API's own code; without the code, the refusal costs one retry from the op's retry budget.
- **D6:** the server now holds the name-to-CID link durably, and that exposure is accepted.

**Rules with no test, or only partly tested:**
- **D6** has no test. It is an exposure statement, so there is no behaviour to test.
- **D3** is partly tested. No test sends a register batch of 1001 entries to the API; only the retire route's entry cap is tested.
- **D4** is partly tested. Every test uses short CIDs, so none reaches the body limit in E1.

**Where the blueprint and the code disagree:**
- **E1 (the bug above):** the API sets no JSON body limit, so the Express default of 100 KiB applies (I confirmed that default in the installed body-parser). A real content CID is 59 characters, so one full entry of 1000 CIDs is about 62 KB. `crates/engine/src/net/register.rs` splits only by entry count. A version of more than about 1,650 blocks (about 1.6 GiB) therefore sends a register request over 100 KiB. The body parser rejects it with a 413 that has no code. Under D5 that costs a retry each time, and the op dead-letters as `AttemptsExhausted`. Files between about 1.6 GiB and the 53.89 GiB flat-DAG ceiling fail this way. The tests miss it because the fake registry checks item counts, not body size, and the contract suite uses short fake CIDs. I found no existing issue for it. The ADR records the blueprint rule.
- **E2 (small):** the blueprint says the caps are checked "before per-item validation". The entry-count and total-target checks are, but the 1000-CID-per-entry check runs during per-item validation. The client gets the same 400 with the code either way.
- **The blueprint is missing part of D5.** It states the registry side (`api.md`), but not the engine rule that a 400 or 413 without a code costs a retry. Consequence 6 adds one sentence to `engine.md` "API client".

**Check against the ADR 0038 draft:** the two agree. My E7 restates ADR 0038 E3: the upload's address-mismatch 400 carries no code, so it costs a retry instead of dead-lettering. Both D5 here and ADR 0038 D4 say `UPLOAD_TOO_LARGE` is permanent.

**Other limits in the Residuals section:**
- **E3:** references are counted per record name, not per version. D1 therefore blocks aliasing across files. Aliasing inside one file still depends on the engine's own `live_node_cids` subtraction.
- **E4:** only the owed-retire path sends the record-scoped retire. Abandon, orphaned head blocks and name waves send the account-wide form.
- **E6:** the operator's TLS edge could forge the refusal code and force a dead letter.

I also added FSM1/cipher-box#898 to "Implemented by", although the audit did not list it. That PR introduced the "act only on the API's own code" rule for 413s.</result>

<summary>Agent "Draft ADR 0048 mailbox ack" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I wrote ADR 0048 to `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0048-the-mailbox-ack-says-whether-this-call-removed-the-item.md` (206 lines). It amends ADR 0023 D6 and cites D5 and E6 for the covered rules without restating them.

**Dn count: 3.**
- **D1:** an idempotency key lives as long as its item. While the item is live, a post with the same key returns that item and the first blob stays. After the ack or the 90-day TTL, the same key creates a new item. This closes the ADR 0023 D6 lag.
- **D2:** a replay of a live item still answers at the pending cap. This is the one gap row. The replay check runs before the cap check, and the replay adds no row.
- **D3:** ADR 0023 D6 now reads under D1. A claim has at most one live item at a time, and a re-post after the ack creates a new item. The owner converts that item as a no-op on a known identity.

Every rule already shipped: the code in FSM1/cipher-box#668 and the blueprint text in FSM1/cipher-box#1962 and FSM1/cipher-box#1981. FSM1/cipher-box#1976 is an agent-written build slice.

**Dn with no test or only partial tests:**
- **D2 (partial):** only the API unit test `lets an idempotent replay through even when the mailbox is full` covers it, on the fake repository. It runs only the path without the lock. No test covers the replay check under the lock at the cap, and neither the real-Postgres integration suite nor the contract suite covers a replay at the cap.
- **D3 (partial):** tests prove that every re-post uses the first key and that the owner holds one live item. No engine test runs the path after the ack: re-post, new item, no-op conversion.
- **D1:** covered by the unit, integration and contract suites. Only the unit suite tests the TTL case.

**Disagreements between the blueprint and the code:**
1. **Order of the owner's ack and the conversion entry.** ADR 0023 D5 and `blueprint/engine.md` "Mailbox logic" say "ack, then write the durable op entry", and the re-post covers a stop between the two. The engine writes the conversion entry before the delete runs (`crates/engine/src/grants/conversion.rs` header, test `a_crash_between_the_ack_and_the_record_write_keeps_the_claim`). So in the code that window does not open. The ADR records the blueprint (E6) and notes that D1 applies in either order.
2. **Seam wording.** The `Mailbox` trait doc in `crates/engine/src/seams/mailbox.rs` and the conformance kit comment say an "already-seen" key creates no second item. That contradicts D1 after the ack. The kit does not assert the after-ack case, so an implementation that never frees a key passes it (E3). The in-memory fake follows D1.
3. **Contract suite coverage.** `blueprint/testing.md` says the contract suite asserts "pending-cap reject-new". `crates/contract/tests/contract.rs` has no pending-cap test (E5).
4. **In-memory fake.** The fake has no pending cap, and it matches a replay on recipient and key only, without the sender. So no engine test exercises D2 (E4).</result>

<result>I wrote the ADR 0043 draft. It has 15 decisions (D1 to D15), and markdownlint passes on it with the repo config. It is about 560 lines, which is over the brief's usual range; ADR 0031 is about 475 lines for 11 decisions.

Output: `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0043-a-delete-writes-the-bin-entry-before-the-unlink-and-every-bin-op-journals-first.md`

**How it fits with ADR 0010 and ADR 0031:**
- **ADR 0010 amendments:** D5 replaces item 3's rule that an unshared scope skips the re-key; now every soft delete re-keys. Only the body of FSM1/cipher-box#1621 gives the reason, so the ADR says the owner accepts this change by accepting the ADR. D9 replaces item 4's "an individual restore mints a fresh key". Items 2, 5, 6 and 7 stand, and the new rules only extend them.
- **ADR 0031:** D14 cites ADR 0031 D9 for the split between a charged verdict and an uncharged hold, and D10 for the stranded mint, without restating them. D14 also records the dead-letter exit that ADR 0031 E7 left open.

**Decisions with no test:**
- **D3:** a settings load with no member choice takes the soft branch.
- **D4:** a child that is a scope root stays a hard delete. FSM1/cipher-box#1618 already said this was untested.

Some other decisions are only partly tested. The missing parts:
- **D2:** an authored retry over a standing entry.
- **D5:** the walk stopping at a descendant scope root.
- **D6:** the per-pass and session capture bounds.
- **D7:** a folder that cannot load holding the op.
- **D10:** the drain reading the `originParent` record, a `deletedAt` mismatch, the stop at a scope root, and a journal write that fails.
- **D11:** only the charge mapping is tested, with no end-to-end dead letter.
- **D12:** the skip rules and the cached read.
- **D13:** the sweep waiting on an unreadable queue.

**Where the blueprint and the code disagree:**
- **E1 (a real disagreement, and a possible data-loss path):** `blueprint/engine.md` "Owner capture" says a later pass re-keys under the standing bin entry's own `deletedAt`. The code does not: `adopt_observed_unlinks` in `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs` always re-keys under the capture's own stamp.
  - When an entry already stands with a different stamp and the node is still sealed under the scope key, the subtree ends up under a key no entry names. That happens when an authored soft delete's entry has landed but its re-key has not, and a grantee unlinks the same node.
  - In that window the authored op is dropped as already satisfied by `rebase_delete`. After that, neither a restore nor a purge can open the subtree.
  - The ADR records the blueprint rule. Nothing tests this window.
- **E2 (a gap the blueprint does not cover):** the command accepts a restore destination in another scope. The drain then dead-letters it as `CrossingUnauthorable` in `publish_restore`, instead of refusing it at the command with `RestoreTargetGone`.

The other residuals, E3 to E8, are limits the ADR states openly:
- **E3:** a focus refresh between the re-key and the unlink reports an unseal rejection.
- **E4:** owner capture only sees unlinks in folders a device has already shown, and a capture is lost if the session ends before it settles.
- **E5:** a scope-root child is destroyed even when retention is above 0.
- **E6:** a withheld bin index holds the head of the op queue with no bound. The hold is reported to the member, but only the record resolving clears it.
- **E7:** a purge that dead-letters keeps its entry, and that entry keeps using room under the bin index size ceiling.
- **E8:** a device that never writes the bin can expire entries late.

I made no git writes and posted nothing to GitHub. Scratch copies of the PR bodies and source files are in `~/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/w0043/`.</result>

<result>**Verdict: RETURN.** Two items need the writer. D1 to D8 check out against the code, and D8 is labelled as proposed.

**Needs the writer**

- **The split with ADR 0033 puts one rule in both ADRs.** ADR 0033 D11 bullet 1 says: "cuts against the lower of that limit and the block ceiling before it mints anything, so only what the uncuttable fields alone overflow is refused." ADR 0042 D1 says: "The target is the lower of the author's limit and the block ceiling (ADR 0033 D11). The produce side refuses only what the typed fields and the uncuttable carried fields (D3, D4) overflow alone." The two refusal clauses also disagree. The 0033 clause has "uncuttable fields alone". The 0042 clause and the code have "typed and uncuttable". Both ADRs also say the cut belongs to 0042 (0033 consequence 4, 0042 consequence 3).
  - Proposed fix: shorten 0033 D11 bullet 1 to "It truncates a carried set and never refuses for it (ADR 0042 D1)". Then 0042 D1 alone owns the target and the refusal clause.
  - The 0033 D-numbers are correct as the draft stands: D8 is the grant-section bound, D9 the charged measures, D10 the headroom arithmetic and D11 the produce-side law. They need no change.
- **Consequence 1 and E3 do not agree.** Consequence 1 says the blueprint "already carries D1 to D7; no text change is needed … the reserved names and `seal.uncuttableKeys` (D3)". E3 says the blueprint "is silent on the names" inside `epochTag`. E3 also finds two more gaps: the budget sentence "every marked field plus `writeSealed`, at both levels" is ambiguous, and "refuses only what the typed fields alone overflow" is correct only when read with the sentences after it. The writer must do one of two things:
  - add an editorial consequence, for example "reserved names at top level" and "typed and uncuttable fields overflow", or
  - say in consequence 1 why no text change is needed despite E3.

**Code checks**

- **D8:** `is_uncuttable` is only the name test or the marker test. Nothing in the carried-field path keeps an "adopted" state. The D8 body ends with "This ADR proposes this answer for the owner to accept".
- **E3:** confirmed. `UNCUTTABLE_KEYS` applies at the top level only. The critical-bytes budget counts only marked keys inside `epochTag`.
- **Publishers:** every production publisher that authors from a carried set calls `report_carried_cut`. These are at `drain.rs:5964` and `rotation.rs:2067`, `:3153` and `:4576`.
- **Gate:** every test named in the Gate exists at origin/main, and so do the three KAT vectors. The "no test" claims for D2 wiring, D6 and D7 are also true.

**Fixed in place**

- Two idioms replaced with plain wording.
- D1: "the encode refuses" became "the produce side refuses". Below the ceiling the encode returns the over-limit block, and the author or the ceiling check refuses it.
- E3: added the `writeSealed` budget ambiguity and the marker's part in the "typed fields alone" reading.
- Gate: replaced "workspace tests" with the real CI check names.

<summary>Agent "Draft ADR 0047 publish retirement" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I wrote the ADR to `/Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0047-a-failed-or-abandoned-publish-retires-exactly-what-it-charged.md`. It has 303 lines and 5 Dn.

The five rules:
- **D1:** an abandoned op retires its name and every block it uploaded.
- **D2:** an op whose record PUT was acknowledged retires nothing.
- **D3:** a publish that fails before the record reaches the network retires its orphaned head block, on each attempt. This also covers the vault settings publish.
- **D4:** a fan-out that no endpoint acknowledged does not count as such a failure.
- **D5:** each publish registers every retained version's root again, and the debt for a dropped version is written to the retire ledger first.

"No pass that journals an entry may decide it" is cited as ADR 0011 D4 and its Context, not restated. ADR 0007 D4 is in Consequences: its one-head-block crash leak stays leaked, because the per-attempt retire keeps its list for one session only.

**Dn with no test:**
- **D3:** nothing tests the guard that stops a head the live record still names from entering the retire list. The rest of D3 is covered.
- **D5:** nothing checks that a publish registers every retained version's root again, at the same name or at a re-key's new name. Only the journal-first half is tested.

**Where the blueprint and the code disagree:**
1. **Which exits count as abandonment (E1).** The blueprint says "An abandoned op retires the whole set" and never says which exits that covers. A spent retry budget on a failure before the PUT does not retire the whole set in the code. It becomes a dead letter that keeps its version and every charged row, and gives back only an unreferenced create's name (`Drain::apply_valve`). This comes from a decision recorded on 2026-08-20 on FSM1/cipher-box#1226 and shipped in FSM1/cipher-box#1343. The audit did not list it. The ADR records the blueprint rule and leaves one call to the owner: add the carve-out as a Dn and to the blueprint, or not.
2. **A permanent leak (E2).** When the member discards a dead letter that keeps its version (`Engine::discard_dead_letter`), or the kept set evicts it (`trim_preserved`), only the local blocks are released. No retire is sent, so the charged registry rows leak for good. This is a leak, not a loss, but nothing bounds it.

**Check against ADR 0046:** the draft appeared while I was writing, so I added cross-references to its D1, D2 and E4. Its E4 agrees with my reasoning for why these retires use the account-wide form. One sentence in its D1 reads loosely: "A retire of a name drops every reference that the name anchored. The pin row goes when no record of the account names the CID." That can read as a name retire also deleting pin rows. In the code, `RegistryService.retire` deletes a pin row only for a CID the batch names as a target. The API test "retiring a name also drops the edges…" passes the leaf as a target too. My E5 states the code behaviour, and D5's safety for leaves depends on it. ADR 0046 D1 should say this more exactly.</result>
