<result>**Verdict: RETURN.** Two issues need the writer. D1–D4, D7 and D8 check out against the code, and all 31 Gate tests exist at the stated paths.

**Needs the writer**

1. **D5 cites the wrong decision source, and E3 misses a real drift.**
   - D5 says the owner decided the rule in FSM1/cipher-box#830. But #830 section 3 decided a different set of nodes: "`Create` → the new node and its parent; `Rename`/`Delete` → the parent (the child's own record is untouched…); `Relink` → both parents; `UpdateContent` → the node alone". It also required one shared function that the overlay and the drain both call.
   - The rule D5 actually states ("Every op but a delete authors its target's next record") first appears in the body of FSM1/cipher-box#878, which an agent wrote. The blueprint (`engine.md:686`) copies that wording.
   - In the code, the overlay stamps the renamed or relinked node (`overlay.rs` `relocate`). The drain's `publish_ref_move` never republishes that node; it publishes only the parent folders, with `modified_at = authored_at` (`drain.rs:4265`). So for rename, relink and move, the overlay stamps a node the drain does not write, and skips the parents the drain does write. That is the jump at publish that #830 exists to prevent.
   - The writer should: re-attribute D5 as NONE (it landed with #878), or record the #830 set and say it contradicts the blueprint. E3 should state the drift instead of "The values agree today because both read the same field". I recommend an issue: the #830 set is the POSIX-correct one, because a rename changes the folder's mtime, not the file's.

2. **E2: amend D6 to record the two-leg plan as a rule, and file an issue for the rollback.**
   - **Amend, not residual.** Keeping the blueprint text and adding a residual is the wrong choice. The staged plan (a parking relink into the vault-root scope, then the arriving op) is the only shape that one drain pass can publish, because a pass carries only one interior end. It is tested by four tests (facade.rs and owner_actions.rs). #1765 gives the reasons: each leg is an existing crossing, the source is cut once, and parking only narrows access.
   - **Proposed D6 wording:** "One kernel rename is one command. A relocation between two interior scopes journals as a parking leg and an arriving leg, and both legs are journaled or neither is."
   - **No owner decision exists.** #1765 is agent-written, closes #1684, and says "No blueprint sentence changes". The ADR must say this, and the owner accepts it with the ADR. `engine.md` "Ops" and `desktop.md:185-188` then need a reword, added as a Consequence.
   - **The rollback is a defect and needs an issue.** `stage_legs_and_notify` removes the parking leg with a best-effort `dequeue_op`. That cannot undo a parking leg that a tick has already drained; the code comment itself allows this case. The result is that the source is cut and the subtree sits in the vault root, while the caller is told the command failed.
   - **Fix:** journal both legs in one atomic write. This needs a multi-entry enqueue on the `StagingStore` seam, because only `enqueue_op` exists today. Add a test that fails the arriving leg's journal.
   - **Parked subtree on another device:** a residual, not a defect. It lasts between two ticks, shows only in the owner's own vault-root scope, and #1765 names it as known.

**Fixed in place:**
- **D1:** added that the drain reads the journaled crossing only to refuse (`drain.rs:4274`, `:4314`).
- **Rationale bullet 2:** narrowed "no observable effect" to the case where the journaling session classified a crossing as intra-scope.
- **D4:** added the third undecodable case, where the clear content root disagrees with the body.
- **E3:** added that `prune`, `restoreVersion` and `deleteVersion` keep the existing `modified_at` in the drain, so the overlay and the drain agree for those three.

<result>## ADR 0032 E4

The 0041 writer is right, and ADR 0032 E4 is half wrong.

- **Grant edits do get the cut-floor check.** Append, invite, permission change and rename go through `edit_scope_set` (`facade.rs:9875`). That calls `publish_edited_set` (`facade.rs:782`), which calls `net.publish_scope_root` (`:812`) on `OwnerRotationNet` (`facade.rs:3451`, `:3778`). Conversion takes the same path (`facade/claim_conversion.rs:289`, `:758`). Next is `OwnerRotationNet::publish_scope_root` (`net/rotation.rs:2147`), then `RootPublish::run` (`:2035`). It takes the lease (`:2044`) and calls `check_publishable` (`:2046`). That check reads the cut-epoch floor (`:2015`) and refuses when the commitment's cut epoch is below it (`:2020`). All of this happens before signing.
- **`grants/invite.rs::check_publishable` checks structure only** (`invite.rs:1106`): the grant-set ceiling, repeated tags, and that the ledger matches the commitment. It runs when the edited set is built (`invite.rs:1089`, `append.rs:225`, `:239`). It is not the last check before signing.
- **The real gap is the `WriteWaveNet` root arm, and only that.** `publish_moved` (`net/rotation.rs:4476`) reads the read-epoch floor (`:4501`) and the write-epoch floor (`:4552`). It then calls `reseal_root` (`:4559`), which re-signs `authorized_commitment` (`:4369`) without reading the cut-epoch floor.

Corrected E4 heading: **"E4 — One owner publish path signs a commitment with no cut-floor read."**

Corrected sentence, replacing "Two paths sign … reads no floor)":

&gt; One path signs a commitment and reads no cut-epoch floor: the root arm of the write wave (`WriteWaveNet::publish_moved` in `net/rotation.rs`), which reads only the read-epoch and write-epoch floors before `reseal_root` re-signs the carried commitment. The grant-edit publishes (append, invite, permission change, rename and conversion) go through `OwnerRotationNet::publish_scope_root` and `RootPublish::run`, so they reach the same cut-epoch check. `grants/invite.rs::check_publishable` is an earlier structural check (the grant-set ceiling, repeated tags, and ledger-commitment agreement), not a floor check.

## Verdict: RETURN

<result>## E1 defect

The defect is real. The threshold is a single register call that names **1,648 or more content CIDs**.

- **Body limit:** `apps/api/src/main.ts:10` calls `NestFactory.create(AppModule, …)` with the default `bodyParser: true`. `configureApp` (`apps/api/src/app-setup.ts:92-104`) sets no `json({ limit })`, and nothing in `apps/api/src` calls `useBodyParser` or `bodyParser`. The installed `@nestjs/platform-express` 11.1.28 registers `express.json(getBodyParserOptions(rawBody))` with no limit (`express-adapter.js:188-196`). body-parser 2.2.2 then falls back to `bytes.parse(options?.limit || '100kb')` (`lib/utils.js:63-65`), which is 102,400 bytes.
- **What the API answers:** body-parser's `PayloadTooLargeError` reaches Nest's global error handler (`routes-resolver.js:84-93`). `handleUnknownError` replies `{statusCode: 413, message}` with no `code` field, and the API registers no global filter.
- **CID length:** `crates/core/src/content/cid.rs:7-16,52,136-145`. A CID is 36 bytes (4 bytes of prefix plus a 32-byte BLAKE3 digest), written as base32 behind a `b`. That is 59 characters, or 62 bytes in the JSON array.
- **Engine split:** `crates/engine/src/net/register.rs:25-49` splits each entry at 1000 CIDs, but chunks the request by entry count only (`REGISTRY_BATCH_MAX = 1000`, `net/mod.rs:28`). A registration of 1,001 to 1,000,000 CIDs therefore goes out as one request. With a 62-character IPNS name, the body is 261 + 62·N bytes: 1,647 CIDs gives 102,375 bytes, and 1,648 gives 102,437, which is over the limit.
- **What N counts:** a file write registers the new root, all of its leaves and the retained roots (`drain.rs:1475-1478,4820-4828`). At the 1,048,536-byte production chunk, that is a file of about 1.6 GiB.
- **Engine classification:** `classify_register` (`drain.rs:6646-6660`) sends a 413 with no code to `Halt::UploadAttempt`, which is charged. After `ATTEMPT_BUDGET = 5` charged passes (`drain.rs:611,1960-1980`), the op dead-letters as `AttemptsExhausted`, about 2.5 minutes in.
- **A second path the writer missed:** a moved record in a rotation wave registers its carry in batches of up to 8,192 CIDs per call (`MOVED_CONTENT_BATCH_CIDS`, `net/rotation.rs:3497,4594-4612`). That is about 509 KB, so any moved file whose retained versions hold 1,648 or more blocks in total also fails. The wave reads the failure as retryable (`WritePublishError::NotLanded`). This path has a lower real-world threshold, because several smaller versions add up.
- **Why no test sees it:** the fake registry checks item counts only (`testkit/account.rs:107-111`). The contract test sends short synthetic CIDs of about 22 characters (`contract.rs:1141`). Staging Caddy caps only the routing PUT (64 KB), not the API.
- **Tracker:** no open issue exists. The given search and wider "body limit" and "100kb" searches return nothing, so one needs to be filed.

I added the exact threshold and the rotation-carry path to E1 in the draft.

## Verdict: RETURN

The other checks passed:
- D1 to D6 match the registry and engine code, after the in-place fixes listed in the review file.
- E2 is confirmed: the `contentCids` `ArrayMaxSize` runs inside `ParseArrayPipe`, after `BatchSizePipe` and `TargetCountPipe`.
- E4 is confirmed: only `send_retire` reaches `retire_for_record`.
- The draft agrees with the ADR 0038 draft's D4 and E3.
- All 34 test names in the Gate section exist at `origin/main`.

Among the in-place fixes, one citation was wrong: E3 cited `live_node_cids`, which FSM1/cipher-box#1588 renamed to `live_owing_record`.

## Needs the writer

- **The per-entry `contentCids` cap is not published as `maxItems`.** The blueprint (`api.md:107-111`) and D3 both say the caps are published as `maxItems` in the OpenAPI document. But in `apps/api/openapi.json`, `RegisterEntryDto.contentCids` has no `maxItems`, and the `@ApiProperty` at `registry.dto.ts:48-51` sets none. The draft needs a residual (or an extension of E2) that states this gap and records the blueprint rule.
- **E1 has no tracker issue.** The orchestrator must file one; E1 can then cite it.
- **Optional, D5 wording.** "A 429, a 401 and a 403 are never charged against the attempt budget" is true. But these answers count against a separate `UNATTRIBUTED_BUDGET = 120` (`drain.rs:613-622,1930-1941`), and that budget also ends in an `AttemptsExhausted` dead letter. A reader could take the sentence to mean these answers never end the op. One added clause fixes it, or it can be left to the candidate that owns dead-letter classification.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Verdict: **RETURN**. The E1 data loss is real, and E2 is a real defect that the draft understates.

## E1 data loss

The loss is real. No open issue tracks it: the requested search returns nothing, and wider searches in all states find nothing relevant.

Trace at `origin/main` c8613750:

1. **The authored path follows the blueprint.** `publish_delete` calls `record_bin_entry` and then `rekey_into_bin` (drain.rs:2867-2872). `record_bin_entry` returns the standing entry's own `deleted_at` (drain.rs:3456).
2. **The capture carries a different stamp.** It is a separate clock read taken when the departure is observed: `observed_at` at focus.rs:167-171, `now.0` at facade.rs:7281.
3. **`adopt_observed_unlinks` ignores the standing entry.** It computes `standing` (drain.rs:3514), then calls `rekey_into_bin(..., unlinked.deleted_at)` whatever `standing` says (drain.rs:3518-3526). With an entry already standing, it writes no entry and drops the capture (drain.rs:3528-3536).
4. **The key comes from that stamp.** `held_key(root, deleted_at)` is taken at drain.rs:3803.
5. **The authored op never finishes the re-key.** There are two routes:
   - `rebase_delete` drops it as `AlreadySatisfied` (rebase.rs:711-712).
   - If the base still holds the node, `publish_delete` finds no link and returns `Ok(())` (drain.rs:2841-2844).
6. **Restore and purge both dead-letter.** Both open under the entry's stamp (drain.rs:2979 and 3059). The failed read goes through `charge_bin_read` (drain.rs:767-772), so both ops dead-letter. The expiry sweep then skips the entry permanently (drain.rs:3600).

**Preconditions (all needed):**
- Retention is above 0, and the node is not a scope root.
- An owner device's soft delete of N publishes the bin entry (stamp T1), but N's root is not re-sealed. Either the first node publish fails, or the pass or session stops between the entry and the re-key.
- Before any retry finishes that re-key, a write grantee unlinks N itself. If the grantee unlinks an ancestor instead, restoring that ancestor brings N back.
- Any owner device observes the unlink, so it holds a capture of N with stamp T2, which differs from T1.
- That device's bin index load resolves and shows the T1 entry, and the re-key under T2 succeeds.

**Result:** N's whole subtree is sealed under key(N, T2), and T2 is kept nowhere. The standing entry names key(N, T1), and no folder links N. Nothing in the code can recover N, and its content stays pinned.

## Needs the writer

- **E1:**
  - File an issue for the loss and cite it as `FSM1/cipher-box#N`.
  - Say "loss" plainly.
  - Add the path:line trace above, including the second route (`publish_delete` returning `Ok(())`).
  - Correct the first bullet. When the authored walk stopped after re-keying N's root, "nothing is lost" is true. But the authored op is also dropped, so the descendants the walk did not reach stay under the scope key. The access cut is incomplete for them, and a purge dead-letters on them (drain.rs:3413-3423).
  - State that the grantee must unlink N itself.
  - Fix direction for the issue: re-key under the standing entry's `deletedAt`, as `record_bin_entry` already does.
- **E2 is a defect.** The code refuses what D9, the blueprint and ADR 0010 item 4 promise:
  - D9: "re-seals every node of the subtree at the destination scope's current epoch".
  - Blueprint: "the destination's grantees read it again by scope membership".
  - ADR 0010 item 4: "Restore into a shared parent re-seals under the destination scope's current epoch".
  - Code: `publish_restore` returns `Halt::Permanent(CrossingUnauthorable)` for any destination in another scope (drain.rs:2973-2978). The facade checks only `contains`, `refuse_outside_vault` and `refuse_full_parent` (facade.rs:8084-8093), so the dead letter comes in place of a `RestoreTargetGone` refusal at command time.

  As a result, a node deleted from an unshared scope cannot be restored into a shared folder. The fix:
  - E2 must say that the code and the blueprint disagree, and which one the ADR records.
  - Consequence 5 ("shared or not") must be limited to a destination in the entry's own scope, or flagged against E2.
  - File an issue; none exists. The fix itself is either a refusal at command time or a cross-scope restore, and that choice is the owner's.
  - One variant to check: if the owner shares the original parent folder after the delete, even a default restore may dead-letter.

- Every candidate-15 row has a Dn.

<task-id>ab381383245406986</task-id>
<tool-use-id>REDACTED</tool-use-id>
<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/1bc4f48e-3cea-46dc-9862-0522f68dd410/tasks/ab381383245406986.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Review ADR 0047 draft" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>RETURN. D5's re-key sentence and E5 get wrong what the name wave registers. E5 also relies on an ADR 0046 D1 sentence that says a name retire deletes more than it does. E2 is a real defect: `Engine::discard_dead_letter`, `trim_preserved` (through `reconcile_preserved_dead_letters`) and `Drain::release_if_refused` all release a charged version and send no retire.

**Needs the writer**

- **D5 re-key sentence and E5 start from a wrong premise.**
  - D5 says: "A write-rotation re-key does the same at the node's new name". E5 says: "A node that moves to a new name registers only the version roots there. The leaves keep the pin rows that their first upload wrote."
  - The code does more than that. `publish_moved` in `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs` registers every version's root and every leaf under the new name before the record moves, through `moved_content_batch`. The test assertion reads "every version root and every leaf reaches the new name". This landed in FSM1/cipher-box#1762.
  - Only a version whose root the wave cannot fetch registers its root alone. `Drain::rekey_subtree` (the bin re-key) keeps the same name.
  - Proposed D5 wording: "A write-rotation name wave registers every version's root and leaves at the node's new name before the record moves (FSM1/cipher-box#1762)." Add FSM1/cipher-box#1762 to "Implemented by".
  - E5 then shrinks to the case where the root cannot be fetched.
  - No blueprint section describes this carry, so Consequence 2 must not claim that "Referenced equals kept" does.
- **E5 cites ADR 0046 D1, and that D1 has its own defect. The writer's claim about it is correct.**
  - 0046 D1 reads: "A retire of a name drops every reference that the name anchored. The pin row goes when no record of the account names the CID." Read together, this says a name retire also deletes pin rows.
  - It does not. `RegistryService.retire` deletes a pin row only for a CID that the batch names as a target (the `held` and `dropCids` sets). The API test "…so its sole leaf unpins" passes only because its batch names the leaf too.
  - The fix to D1 goes to the 0046 writer; the proposed text is in the review file.
  - Until then, 0047 E5 should cite `RegistryService.retire` alone.

**E2, ready to file**

- **Where it happens:**
  - `Engine::discard_dead_letter` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:12648`).
  - `trim_preserved`, whose evicted roots `reconcile_preserved_dead_letters` releases (`crates/engine/src/sync/staging.rs:517`, `:891`).
  - `Drain::release_if_refused` (`crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs:6221`). It is reached from the spent-budget arm of `apply_valve` and from `abandon_keeping_its_name` when the preserved set refuses the version.
- **What goes wrong:** each of these calls `release_version_blocks` and sends no retire. That drops the only manifest that lists the charged CIDs.
- **Who is affected:** dead letters that keep their rows. These are a spent attempt budget, `BaseSuperseded`, `AlreadyPublished` and a spent unattributed budget. `Preservation::ContentGone` leaks the same way.
- **Shape of the fix:**
  - Before any of these releases, read the CID set with `version_leaf_cids` plus the root.
  - Hand it to a durable retire that subtracts what the node's live record still names (`live_node_cids`, record-scoped). Then release.
  - Do not send a blind account-wide `retire`. The attempt count is per op, not per kind of failure, so an earlier acknowledged PUT may have left a live record that names the version. Also, the trim runs outside a drain pass.
- **Two gaps in the retire ledger to close:**
  - The ledger stores only the root and fetches the leaves from the network when it settles. A root that never uploaded can never be expanded, so its entry stays owed permanently. Either store the leaf list or retire inline inside a drain pass.
  - For a create that never published, `OwingRecord::Published` treats the missing record as unreadable and never retires the entry. It needs the class that treats a missing record as an empty live set.
- **Custody:** the trim may journal only entries this session's keys can open. It must keep any other entry rather than release it.

I also fixed smaller errors in the draft in place: E1's list of exits that retire the whole set was wrong, D3's live-set check applies only in the drain, and one D5 gate claim was wrong. The list is in the review file. All 20 named tests exist at `origin/main`. Both lint runs pass.

Files:
- /Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/review-0047.md
- /Users/myankelev/.claude/scratch/close-635/adrs/0047-a-failed-or-abandoned-publish-retires-exactly-what-it-charged.md</result>
